{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_658c4f2a857b4dd7954f6485","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Workers payload type contract — plan\n2\t\n3\tIssue: #1455\n4\tBranch: `feat/workers-payload-type-contract`\n5\tBaseline: `ec848e6b0334ec8fcd2bc66ba009305d35367b01` (`origin/main`)\n6\tLane: Codex · OpenAI · GPT-5.6 Sol · high (`complex_implementation`)\n7\t\n8\t## Decision\n9\t\n10\tMake the payload schema the single application-owned definition of a job payload. Its output type\n11\tflows through `JobDefinition`, generated job registries, the workers `triggerJob` contract, and\n12\ttrigger-core `enqueueJob`; the same schema validates the payload immediately before the job handler\n13\truns. The queue message and enqueue/dispatch sequence do not change.\n14\t\n15\tThis is deliberately source-breaking for schema-less payload declarations and schema-less job\n16\thandlers. Keeping `.payload<T>()` or `defineJobHandler(handler)` as a silent fallback would preserve\n17\tthe defect: the generator could recover a compile-time annotation at best, but there would be no\n18\truntime definition with which to prevent producer/consumer drift.\n19\t\n20\t## Scope and doctrine\n21\t\n22\t- `packages/plugin-workers-core`: Archetype 3 (Runtime/Behavior), current doctrine verdict\n23\t  **Refactor**. The builder is a folded DSL concern, not a separate package archetype.\n24\t- `packages/plugin-triggers-core`: Archetype 3 (Runtime/Behavior), current doctrine verdict\n25\t  **Keep**.\n26\t- `plugins/workers`: Archetype 5 (Plugin Package), current doctrine verdict **Refactor**. The plugin\n27\t  remains thin: payload/schema/registry vocabulary is owned by workers-core; the plugin generator\n28\t  only composes and emits it.\n29\t- Scope overlays: none. This does not change an application service implementation, frontend, or\n30\t  documentation-only surface.\n31\t- In-scope anti-pattern checks: AP-1, AP-3, AP-8, AP-9, AP-10, AP-11, AP-13, AP-14, AP-16, AP-19,\n32\t  AP-20, AP-22, AP-23, AP-24, and AP-25. In particular, generated `JobHandler<any>` and its blanket\n33\t  lint suppression must disappear rather than move.\n34\t- Relevant existing debt: `packages/plugin-workers-core` contract/domain cardinality and\n35\t  `plugins/workers` connector split remain open but must not be deepened. The 20-diagnostic\n36\t  `plugins/workers` private-type-ref baseline under `workers-private-type-ref-1655` is pre-existing\n37\t  and is a strict no-increase allowance, not a pass. No new debt is planned.\n38\t\n39\t## Current published surface (`deno doc` at baseline)\n40\t\n41\tThe following facts were read from `deno doc`, not inferred from implementation files.\n42\t\n43\t| Entry point | Published symbol | Baseline signature relevant to #1455 |\n44\t| --- | --- | --- |\n45\t| `@netscript/plugin-workers-core` | `JobDefinition` | `JobDefinition<TId extends string = string> = Readonly<{ id; entrypoint?; name?; topic? }>` |\n46\t| root | `JobBuilder` | `payload<TNextPayload>(): JobBuilder<..., TNextPayload, ...>`; `build(): JobDefinition<TId>` |\n47\t| root | `defineJob` | `defineJob<TId>(id): JobBuilder<TId, \"initial\", unknown, unknown>` |\n48\t| root | `defineJobHandler` | `defineJobHandler<TPayload = unknown>(handler): (context: JobHandlerContext<TPayload>) => JobResult<unknown> \\| Promise<...>` |\n49\t| `./builders` | `JobDefinition` | already `JobDefinition<TId = string, TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` |\n50\t| `./builders` | `JobBuilder` | already returns `JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>`, but `payload<T>()` is type-only |\n51\t| `./runtime` | `JobDefinition` | already has `<TId, TPayload, TResult>` and optional `handler`, but no payload schema |\n52\t| `./runtime` | `StaticJobRegistry` | `ReadonlyMap<string, JobHandler>` (default payload `unknown`) |\n53\t| `./contracts/v1` | `JobTriggerInput` | non-generic `{ id?: string; payload?: Record<string, unknown>; ... }` |\n54\t| `./contracts/v1` | `WorkersContract` | non-generic alias of the real `WorkersContractDefinition` |\n55\t| `./contracts/v1` | `workersContract` | non-generic client-generation contract value |\n56\t| `./contracts/v1` | `workersContractV1` / `WorkersContractV1` | non-generic context-bindable service implementer |\n57\t| `@netscript/plugin-triggers-core` | `EnqueueJobOptions` | `EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload = unknown>`, with `payload?: TPayload` |\n58\t| root and `./builders` | `EnqueueJobAction` | `job: JobDefinition<TJobId>` while `options` separately carries `TPayload` |\n59\t| root and `./builders` | `enqueueJob` | `<TJobId, TPayload>(job: JobDefinition<TJobId>, options: EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>)` |\n60\t\n61\tThe root workers `JobDefinition` is distinct from the richer builders/domain/runtime types with the\n62\tsame name. The root wrapper is the erasing boundary used by trigger-core today.\n63\t\n64\t## 1. Exact proposed public shapes\n65\t\n66\t### Workers payload schema and handler definition\n67\t\n68\tWorkers-core owns a structural Standard Schema surface; it does not re-export Zod or another\n69\tupstream schema library.\n70\t\n71\t```ts\n72\texport type JobPayloadSchema<TPayload = unknown> = PublicStandardSchema<TPayload>;\n73\t\n74\texport type JobHandler<TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown> = (\n75\t  context: JobHandlerContext<TPayload>,\n76\t) => JobResult<TResult> | Promise<JobResult<TResult>>;\n77\t\n78\texport type JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown> =\n79\t  & JobHandler<TPayload, TResult>\n80\t  & Readonly<{ payloadSchema: JobPayloadSchema<TPayload> }>;\n81\t\n82\texport function defineJobHandler<TPayload, TResult = unknown>(\n83\t  payloadSchema: JobPayloadSchema<TPayload>,\n84\t  handler: JobHandler<TPayload, TResult>,\n85\t): JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload, TResult>;\n86\t```\n87\t\n88\t`defineJobHandler` returns a callable wrapper. The callback receives the schema output type. The\n89\twrapper validates `context.payload` with the attached schema, replaces the callback context payload\n90\twith the validated output, and then invokes the callback. It throws a workers validation error on\n91\tschema issues. This changes validation only; job selection, queueing, retry, priority, correlation,\n92\tand handler result semantics are unchanged.\n93\t\n94\t### Job definition and builder\n95\t\n96\tThe root surface is aligned with the already-published builders/runtime generic order. Defaults keep\n97\ttype references such as `JobDefinition<'health-check'>` valid.\n98\t\n99\t```ts\n100\texport type JobDefinition<\n101\t  TId extends string = string,\n102\t  TPayload = unknown,\n103\t  TResult = unknown,\n104\t> = Readonly<{\n105\t  id: JobId<TId>;\n106\t  entrypoint?: string;\n107\t  name?: string;\n108\t  topic?: string;\n109\t  payloadSchema?: JobPayloadSchema<TPayload>;\n110\t  handler?: JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload, TResult>;\n111\t}>;\n112\t\n113\texport interface JobBuilder<\n114\t  TId extends string,\n115\t  TConfigured extends \"initial\" | \"entrypoint-set\" | \"handler-set\",\n116\t  TPayload,\n117\t  TResult,\n118\t> {\n119\t  payload<TNextPayload>(\n120\t    schema: JobPayloadSchema<TNextPayload>,\n121\t  ): JobBuilder<TId, TConfigured, TNextPayload, TResult>;\n122\t\n123\t  handler<TNextPayload = TPayload, TNextResult = TResult>(\n124\t    fn: JobHandler<TNextPayload, TNextResult>,\n125\t  ): JobBuilder<TId, \"handler-set\", TNextPayload, TNextResult>;\n126\t\n127\t  build(): JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>; // existing typestate `this` guard retained\n128\t}\n129\t```\n130\t\n131\tThe richer `./builders` and `./runtime` `JobDefinition` shapes gain the same optional\n132\t`payloadSchema` field. Optionality preserves manually-authored/registry-loaded legacy definitions\n133\tthat never declared a payload. Once `.payload(schema)` is called, the builder stores that schema and\n134\tthe built value carries it. The no-argument `.payload<T>()` overload is not retained.\n135\t\n136\t### Payload extraction and literal registry algebra\n137\t\n138\t```ts\n139\texport type JobPayloadOf<TDefinition> =\n140\t  TDefinition extends JobDefinition<string, infer TPayload, unknown>\n141\t    ? TPayload\n142\t    : TDefinition extends JobHandlerDefinition<infer TPayload, unknown>\n143\t      ? TPayload\n144\t      : never;\n145\t\n146\texport type JobPayloadMap<\n147\t  TRegistry extends Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>,\n148\t> = Readonly<{\n149\t  [TId in keyof TRegistry]: JobPayloadOf<TRegistry[TId]>;\n150\t}>;\n151\t```\n152\t\n153\tThe workers generator emits a literal object before it widens values into runtime `Map`s:\n154\t\n155\t```ts\n156\texport const jobHandlersById = {\n157\t  \"embed-document\": resolveJobHandler(job0, \"workers/jobs/embed-document.ts\"),\n158\t  \"transcribe-image\": resolveJobHandler(job1, \"workers/jobs/transcribe-image.ts\"),\n159\t} as const;\n160\t\n161\texport const jobDefinitionsById = {\n162\t  \"embed-document\": createLocalJobDefinition(\n163\t    \"embed-document\",\n164\t    \"./embed-document.ts\",\n165\t    jobHandlersById[\"embed-document\"],\n166\t  ),\n167\t  \"transcribe-image\": createLocalJobDefinition(\n168\t    \"transcribe-image\",\n169\t    \"./transcribe-image.ts\",\n170\t    jobHandlersById[\"transcribe-image\"],\n171\t  ),\n172\t} as const;\n173\t\n174\texport type GeneratedJobDefinitionRegistry = typeof jobDefinitionsById;\n175\texport type GeneratedJobPayloadMap = JobPayloadMap<GeneratedJobDefinitionRegistry>;\n176\t\n177\t// Existing runtime exports remain Maps and retain their names.\n178\texport const registry: StaticJobRegistry = new Map(/* literal handler entries */);\n179\texport const jobDefinitions: ReadonlyMap<string, RegisterJobInput> =\n180\t  new Map(/* serializable operational definitions */);\n181\texport const definitions = jobDefinitions;\n182\t```\n183\t\n184\tFor that generated type:\n185\t\n186\t```ts\n187\ttype GeneratedJobPayloadMap = Readonly<{\n188\t  \"embed-document\": EmbedDocumentPayload;\n189\t  \"transcribe-image\": TranscribeImagePayload;\n190\t}>;\n191\t```\n192\t\n193\t`createLocalJobDefinition`/`createConfiguredJobDefinition` remain responsible for the post-#1872\n194\toperational fields. They additionally carry the handler's schema in the in-process literal\n195\tdefinition; the existing serializable registration map keeps the existing operational projection.\n196\tThis does not redesign metadata precedence or absorb #1451.\n197\t\n198\t### Typed workers `triggerJob` contract\n199\t\n200\t```ts\n201\texport type JobPayloadRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;\n202\t\n203\ttype JobTriggerCommon = Readonly<{\n204\t  priority?: number;\n205\t  delay?: number;\n206\t  correlationId?: string;\n207\t  traceparent?: string;\n208\t  tracestate?: string;\n209\t}>;\n210\t\n211\ttype PayloadField<TPayload> = undefined extends TPayload\n212\t  ? Readonly<{ payload?: TPayload }>\n213\t  : Readonly<{ payload: TPayload }>;\n214\t\n215\texport type JobTriggerInput<\n216\t  TPayloads extends JobPayloadRecord = JobPayloadRecord,\n217\t> = string extends keyof TPayloads\n218\t  ? Readonly<JobTriggerCommon & {\n219\t      id?: string;\n220\t      payload?: Record<string, unknown>;\n221\t    }>\n222\t  : {\n223\t      [TId in keyof TPayloads & string]: Readonly<\n224\t        JobTriggerCommon & { id: TId } & PayloadField<TPayloads[TId]>\n225\t      >;\n226\t    }[keyof TPayloads & string];\n227\t\n228\texport type WorkersContract<\n229\t  TPayloads extends JobPayloadRecord = JobPayloadRecord,\n230\t> = WorkersContractDefinition<TPayloads>;\n231\t\n232\texport function createWorkersContract<\n233\t  TPayloads extends JobPayloadRecord = JobPayloadRecord,\n234\t>(): WorkersContract<TPayloads>;\n235\t```\n236\t\n237\t`WorkersContractDefinition<TPayloads>` remains the real oRPC contract type; only its `triggerJob`\n238\troute input schema type is parameterized. `createWorkersContract()` returns the same runtime\n239\tcontract definition as `workersContract`, with the generic narrowing proven safe because every map\n240\tvalue is constrained to the route's existing record payload domain. This is the canonical published\n241\tsurface, not an EIS-Chat compatibility shim.\n242\t\n243\tThe existing `workersContract`, `workersContractV1`, and `WorkersContractV1` remain the broad default\n244\tservice implementation surfaces. Services must implement the whole runtime route, while a client\n245\topts into its application registry:\n246\t\n247\t```ts\n248\tconst contract = createWorkersContract<GeneratedJobPayloadMap>();\n249\tconst client = createServiceClient({ contract, serviceName: \"workers\" });\n250\t\n251\tawait client.triggerJob({\n252\t  id: \"embed-document\",\n253\t  payload: { documentId: \"doc-1\", text: \"...\" },\n254\t});\n255\t\n256\tawait client.triggerJob({\n257\t  id: \"transcribe-image\",\n258\t  // @ts-expect-error EmbedDocumentPayload is not TranscribeImagePayload.\n259\t  payload: { documentId: \"doc-1\", text: \"...\" },\n260\t});\n261\t```\n262\t\n263\t### Trigger-core enqueue binding\n264\t\n265\t```ts\n266\texport type EnqueueJobAction<\n267\t  TJobId extends string = string,\n268\t  TPayload = unknown,\n269\t> = Readonly<{\n270\t  kind: \"enqueue-job\";\n271\t  job: JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>;\n272\t  jobId: JobId<TJobId>;\n273\t  options: EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>;\n274\t}>;\n275\t\n276\texport function enqueueJob<TJobId extends string, TPayload = unknown>(\n277\t  job: JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>,\n278\t  options: EnqueueJobOptions<NoInfer<TPayload>>,\n279\t): EnqueueJobAction<TJobId, TPayload>;\n280\t```\n281\t\n282\t`NoInfer` is essential. Without it TypeScript may infer or widen `TPayload` from both arguments,\n283\trecreating the independent-payload defect as a union/escape. The selected definition is the only\n284\tinference source; the options payload is checked against it.\n285\t\n286\t## 2. Published signature changes and source compatibility\n287\t\n288\t| Published symbol | Proposed change | Source-breaking? |\n289\t| --- | --- | --- |\n290\t| root `JobDefinition` | Add `TPayload = unknown`, `TResult = unknown`, optional schema and handler fields | **No for type references** because defaults preserve `JobDefinition` and `JobDefinition<TId>`; optional fields preserve structural values. Code depending on the old type having exactly one generic parameter only through a higher-order generic alias may need adjustment, but normal uses remain valid. |\n291\t| root `JobBuilder.payload` | Replace `.payload<T>()` with `.payload(schema)` and infer `T` from the schema | **Yes.** Every schema-less call must supply its runtime schema. This is required to close drift. |\n292\t| root `JobBuilder.build` | Return `JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>` instead of `JobDefinition<TId>` | **No for correct consumers; intentional tightening downstream.** Payload/result information is additive until a caller passes an incompatible payload. |\n293\t| root `defineJobHandler` | Require `(payloadSchema, handler)` and return `JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload, TResult>` | **Yes.** One-argument handler declarations must pass their existing schema (or introduce one). This is how generation recovers both type and validator. |\n294\t| new root symbols `JobPayloadSchema`, `JobHandler`, `JobHandlerDefinition`, `JobPayloadOf`, `JobPayloadMap` | Add contract algebra | No; additive. |\n295\t| `./builders` `JobDefinition` | Add optional `payloadSchema` to the underlying domain shape | No; additive optional field with existing generic defaults/order. |\n296\t| `./builders` `JobBuilder.payload` | Require schema argument | **Yes**, same migration as root. |\n297\t| `./runtime` `JobDefinition` | Add optional `payloadSchema` | No; additive optional field. |\n298\t| `./runtime` `StaticJobRegistry` | Use a payload-erased existential handler (`JobHandler<never, unknown>`) instead of `JobHandler<unknown, unknown>` so heterogeneous typed handlers can be stored without `any` | **Potentially yes.** Code that reads a static handler and invokes it without first correlating it with a definition will stop compiling. Construction from specifically typed handlers becomes valid. Runtime dispatch already performs the correlation/cast at the registry boundary. |\n299\t| `./contracts/v1` `JobTriggerInput` | Add payload-map generic and a literal-id discriminated union; retain broad default branch | No for existing `JobTriggerInput` uses because the default reproduces the old optional `id`/record payload shape. **Opted-in typed uses intentionally reject** missing ids and mismatched payloads. |\n300\t| `./contracts/v1` `WorkersContract` | Add payload-map generic with broad default | No for existing uses; default preserves current type. |\n301\t| new `./contracts/v1` `createWorkersContract` | Add canonical typed client-contract factory | No; additive. |\n302\t| `workersContract`, `workersContractV1`, `WorkersContractV1` | Keep broad default signatures | No change. The service implementer is not application-registry-specific. |\n303\t| trigger-core `EnqueueJobAction` | Carry payload generic into its `job` field | No for existing references because generic defaults remain. |\n304\t| trigger-core `enqueueJob` | Infer payload from `JobDefinition<TId, TPayload>` and apply `NoInfer` to options | **Yes, intentionally, for incorrect calls.** A payload belonging to another definition stops compiling. Correct calls and untyped legacy `JobDefinition<TId>` calls retain behavior (`TPayload = unknown`). Explicit generic calls whose declared payload disagrees with the definition also stop compiling. |\n305\t| generated module exports | Add literal `jobHandlersById`, `jobDefinitionsById`, and generated type aliases; preserve `registry`, `jobDefinitions`, and `definitions` Map exports | No for supported exported names. Consumers parsing the generated source text or relying on the non-public internal `jobHandlers` array must update. |\n306\t| generated job source convention | Require every `defineJobHandler` call to supply a Standard Schema | **Yes.** Existing handler modules must pass the schema they commonly already use manually. Schema-less arbitrary function exports cannot provide the promised id→payload contract and are rejected by generation with a path-specific error. |\n307\t\n308\t## 3. Binding rules\n309\t\n310\t### Workers service `triggerJob`\n311\t\n312\tThe generated `GeneratedJobPayloadMap` is passed to `createWorkersContract`. The generic contract's\n313\t`triggerJob` input is a discriminated union keyed by literal `id`; each union member's `payload` is\n314\tthe mapped payload for only that id. The broad `workersContractV1` service implementer remains\n315\tunchanged and receives the same wire object. No router selection, HTTP path, queue, or persistence\n316\tlogic changes.\n317\t\n318\t### Trigger-core `enqueueJob`\n319\t\n320\tThe function infers `TId` and `TPayload` exclusively from the selected `JobDefinition`. `NoInfer` on\n321\t`EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>` turns the second argument into a checking position. The action retains\n322\tboth generic arguments, so later trigger processors cannot lose the correlation internally.\n323\t\n324\t## 4. Generated registration boundary\n325\t\n326\tToday the manifest forces `mapValueType: \"JobHandler<any>\"`, adds a file-wide\n327\t`no-explicit-any` suppression, puts handlers into a homogeneous array, and only then builds the\n328\tregistry. That is the exact erasure point.\n329\t\n330\tThe generator will instead:\n331\t\n332\t1. import each job module as it does now;\n333\t2. resolve each module with a generic `resolveJobHandler<TModule>(module, path)` whose conditional\n334\t   return type preserves the concrete default/named handler type;\n335\t3. emit `jobHandlersById` as a literal `as const` object, before any `Map` widening;\n336\t4. emit `jobDefinitionsById` from those exact handler values, preserving each attached\n337\t   `payloadSchema` type while applying the existing #1451 operational policy projection;\n338\t5. derive `GeneratedJobPayloadMap` with workers-core `JobPayloadMap`;\n339\t6. project the literal objects into the existing runtime `registry`, `jobDefinitions`, and\n340\t   `definitions` maps;\n341\t7. remove `mapValueType: \"JobHandler<any>\"` and the generated lint suppression.\n342\t\n343\tBoth workers registry emitters (`runtime-registry-generator.ts` and the package-owned\n344\t`registry-compiler.ts`) must emit the same contract. Their golden/semantic tests will assert literal\n345\tkeys, absence of `any`/lint suppression, preservation of existing operational definition fields,\n346\tand compilation of the generated module.\n347\t\n348\t## 5. One definition for runtime validation\n349\t\n350\tThe Standard Schema passed to `.payload(schema)` or `defineJobHandler(schema, handler)` owns all\n351\tthree views:\n352\t\n353\t1. schema output infers the handler callback payload;\n354\t2. generated `JobPayloadMap` exposes that same output to producers (`triggerJob` and `enqueueJob`);\n355\t3. the returned/built handler definition carries that schema and validates the unknown wire payload\n356\t   immediately before the application handler runs.\n357\t\n358\tThe generator never parses TypeScript syntax and never asks an application to duplicate a payload\n359\tinterface. It only transports the typed handler/definition value. A Zod schema, Valibot schema, or\n360\tother Standard Schema implementation works through the package-owned structural contract.\n361\t\n362\tRuntime validation happens at the handler boundary rather than rewriting the enqueue route. An\n363\tinvalid untyped producer can still place the same message on the queue, but the consumer rejects it\n364\tbefore application code. Typed producers cannot construct the mismatched `triggerJob`/`enqueueJob`\n365\tcall in the first place. This preserves enqueue ordering, delay, priority, correlation, idempotency,\n366\tand retry behavior.\n367\t\n368\t## 6. Defaults and what cannot be preserved\n369\t\n370\tDefaults:\n371\t\n372\t- `JobDefinition<TId = string, TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` preserves zero- and one-\n373\t  argument references.\n374\t- `JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` and payload helper types default to\n375\t  `unknown` for generic library code.\n376\t- `EnqueueJobAction<TId = string, TPayload = unknown>` preserves existing references.\n377\t- `JobTriggerInput<JobPayloadRecord>` and `WorkersContract<JobPayloadRecord>` reproduce the current\n378\t  broad service/client surface when no application map is supplied.\n379\t- Legacy manually-created `JobDefinition<TId>` values remain structurally valid because\n380\t  `payloadSchema` and `handler` are optional. They remain untyped (`unknown`) and therefore cannot\n381\t  claim the new invariant.\n382\t\n383\tCannot be preserved:\n384\t\n385\t- Schema-less `.payload<T>()`: a type argument disappears at runtime, so retaining it would violate\n386\t  the single-definition requirement.\n387\t- One-argument `defineJobHandler(handler)`: without an attached schema the generator cannot produce\n388\t  runtime validation from the same definition.\n389\t- Arbitrary generated job modules found only by “first function export”: that heuristic erases the\n390\t  handler contract. Generated jobs must export a schema-backed handler as `default` or `handler`.\n391\t- Mismatched payload calls: rejecting them is the acceptance criterion and an intentional source\n392\t  break.\n393\t- Invoking a value read directly from heterogeneous `StaticJobRegistry` without a correlated job\n394\t  definition: the existential registry type makes that unsafe operation uncallable.\n395\t- Typed `triggerJob` calls that omit the literal id because an HTTP path will add it later: the id is\n396\t  the discriminant at a TypeScript call site and must be present. The broad service implementer\n397\t  still accepts the path-populated shape.\n398\t- Consumers that inspect exact generated source text or private intermediate arrays: generated code\n399\t  is rewritten to preserve literal types. Stable runtime export names remain.\n400\t\n401\t## RED → GREEN proof\n402\t\n403\tThe RED commit adds a consumer-site type test using two real schema-backed definitions:\n404\t\n405\t```ts\n406\ttype EmbedDocumentPayload = { documentId: string; text: string };\n407\ttype TranscribeImagePayload = { imageUrl: string; language?: string };\n408\t\n409\tconst embedDocument = defineJob(\"embed-document\")\n410\t  .payload(EmbedDocumentPayloadSchema)\n411\t  .handler(/* correctly typed handler */)\n412\t  .build();\n413\t\n414\tconst transcribeImage = defineJob(\"transcribe-image\")\n415\t  .payload(TranscribeImagePayloadSchema)\n416\t  .handler(/* correctly typed handler */)\n417\t  .build();\n418\t\n419\tenqueueJob(embedDocument, { payload: embedPayload }); // positive control\n420\tenqueueJob(transcribeImage, {\n421\t  // @ts-expect-error EmbedDocumentPayload does not belong to transcribe-image.\n422\t  payload: embedPayload,\n423\t});\n424\t```\n425\t\n426\tAt RED, the check fails with “Unused `@ts-expect-error` directive”, proving current main accepts the\n427\twrong payload. It must not fail because of a missing import, invalid job definition, or unrelated\n428\tschema error. GREEN is the same fixture compiling with the directive consumed. A second fixture\n429\tchecks `createWorkersContract<GeneratedJobPayloadMap>().triggerJob` with the same pair. Both commit\n430\tSHAs are recorded in `worklog.md`.\n431\t\n432\t## Commit slices\n433\t\n434\t1. **Plan contract** — this file only. Gate: human/PLAN-EVAL review. Files: `plan.md`.\n435\t2. **RED consumer proof** — add positive and negative compile-time fixtures without changing source.\n436\t   Gate: targeted check fails only on unused `@ts-expect-error`. Files: workers/trigger-core type\n437\t   test(s), `worklog.md`, `context-pack.md`.\n438\t3. **Core type carrier** — schema-backed handler/definition/builder shapes, payload map algebra,\n439\t   typed workers contract, and enqueue binding. Gate: targeted package checks/tests turn RED green.\n440\t   Files under `packages/plugin-workers-core`, `packages/plugin-triggers-core`, plus run artifacts.\n441\t4. **Generated application boundary** — both registry emitters, manifest, scaffold/built-in job\n442\t   declarations, golden and semantic generated-code tests. Gate: plugin package tests and generated\n443\t   module check. Files under `plugins/workers`, plus run artifacts.\n444\t5. **Merge-readiness gates and evidence** — no product changes unless a gate finds an in-scope\n445\t   defect. Gate set below; update only run artifacts/PR evidence.\n446\t\n447\t## Gate set\n448\t\n449\t- Scoped `run-deno-check.ts --root <root> --ext ts,tsx` for each touched root, with `--unstable-kv`\n450\t  supplied through the wrapper/toolchain where applicable.\n451\t- Each touched package/plugin test suite through `run-deno-test.ts` or its package task.\n452\t- Root `deno task check` and `deno task test`, because published cross-package types change.\n453\t- Scoped `run-deno-lint.ts` and `run-deno-fmt.ts` for each touched root.\n454\t- `deno task quality:scan` / `deno task quality:gate` (framework-source requirement).\n455\t- Full-export `deno task doc:lint` for touched published units. The workers plugin's recorded 20\n456\t  private-type-ref diagnostics must not increase or gain another diagnostic class.\n457\t- JSR package audit for every touched published unit.\n458\t- `deno task publish:dry-run`.\n459\t- `deno task arch:check`.\n460\t- Consumer compilation of generated registry plus triggerJob/enqueueJob type fixtures.\n461\t- Runtime tests proving schema validation occurs before handler application without changing\n462\t  enqueue message construction or action shape.\n463\t\n464\tThe full `scaffold.runtime` E2E is not selected initially: this plan changes generated TypeScript but\n465\tnot scaffold installation, DB wiring, Aspire helper generation, or runtime resource topology. If\n466\tthe generator tests cannot prove the emitted module in a real scaffold, that is a rescope trigger\n467\tand the canonical one-pass suite becomes required.\n468\t\n469\t## Risk register\n470\t\n471\t| Risk | Mitigation |\n472\t| --- | --- |\n473\t| TypeScript infers payload from both `job` and `options`, allowing a union | Put `NoInfer` on the options position and lock it with the wrong-job compile fixture. |\n474\t| Contract typing becomes another hand-authored oRPC shim | Parameterize the existing real `WorkersContractDefinition`; keep runtime schema/value identical and run contract soundness tests. |\n475\t| Standard Schema inference exposes upstream types or slow types | Publish a workers-owned structural alias, run full doc-lint and publish dry-run. |\n476\t| Generated literal typing is widened before extraction | Emit `as const` objects first; derive aliases before projecting to `Map`. Compile the emitted module. |\n477\t| `any` moves from manifest to a cast/helper | Remove the preamble and run `quality:scan`; no `any`, blanket lint ignore, or `as unknown as` is allowed. |\n478\t| Runtime validation accidentally changes enqueue/retry behavior | Validate only inside the schema-backed handler wrapper and compare action/message construction tests. |\n479\t| #1451 operational registry metadata is regressed | Preserve `RegisterJobInput` projection and policy-precedence golden assertions; do not redesign generated operational fields. |\n480\t| Existing worker modules break without a usable migration | Update first-party/stub/sample modules in the same slice and document the mechanical migration: pass the schema already used for parsing to `defineJobHandler`. |\n481\t| Pre-existing workers doc-lint debt hides a regression | Record baseline diagnostic count/class separately and require no increase; all other touched units must be zero. |\n482\t\n483\t## Open-decision sweep\n484\t\n485\t- **Resolved now:** schema-less payload APIs are source-breaking and removed. Deferring this would\n486\t  force generator and runtime rewrites later.\n487\t- **Resolved now:** runtime validation occurs at the handler boundary; enqueue semantics remain\n488\t  unchanged.\n489\t- **Resolved now:** the broad service implementer stays broad; application clients opt into the\n490\t  literal map with `createWorkersContract<...>()`.\n491\t- **Resolved now:** generated runtime Map export names remain stable; new literal objects/type aliases\n492\t  are additive.\n493\t- **Safe to defer:** a future input/output split for Standard Schema defaults/transforms. This issue\n494\t  maps schema output as the payload contract; supporting distinct producer input and handler output\n495\t  types can add an additional generic later without changing this id correlation.\n496\t- **Safe to defer:** task/workflow payload parity. #1455 is job-specific; copying the change to task\n497\t  or workflow builders without a named consumer invariant would expand scope.\n498\t\n499\tNo must-resolve decision remains open before implementation.\n500\t\n501\t## Explicit non-goals\n502\t\n503\t- #1451 operational metadata design or any alternate job discovery/loading system.\n504\t- A temporary EIS-Chat/shared-contract adapter or other consumer-side compatibility shim.\n505\t- Rewriting queueing, scheduling, retries, idempotency, correlation, service routing, or worker\n506\t  dispatch.\n507\t- Task/workflow payload mapping.\n508\t- Closing unrelated doctrine debt or the workers private-type-ref issue.\n509\t\n510\t## Contributor path\n511\t\n512\tA contributor adds a job by defining one Standard Schema next to the handler, passing it to\n513\t`defineJobHandler(schema, handler)`, and exporting that definition as `default` or `handler`.\n514\tRunning the existing workers registry generator emits the literal id entry, payload-map alias,\n515\truntime handler Map, and operational definition Map. Producers import/re-export the generated\n516\t`GeneratedJobPayloadMap` and instantiate `createWorkersContract<GeneratedJobPayloadMap>()`; trigger\n517\tbuilders pass the built `JobDefinition` directly to `enqueueJob`.\n518\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5458dd52-4a67-46ac-a724-a61805794047","uuid":"266d50e3-2dec-4ef7-97c9-486350fd770d","timestamp":"2026-09-02T16:03:51.214Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/agent/projects/netscript/worktrees/007-leaf-1455/.llm/runs/workers-payload-type-contract--plan/plan.md","content":"# Workers payload type contract — plan\n\nIssue: #1455\nBranch: `feat/workers-payload-type-contract`\nBaseline: `ec848e6b0334ec8fcd2bc66ba009305d35367b01` (`origin/main`)\nLane: Codex · OpenAI · GPT-5.6 Sol · high (`complex_implementation`)\n\n## Decision\n\nMake the payload schema the single application-owned definition of a job payload. Its output type\nflows through `JobDefinition`, generated job registries, the workers `triggerJob` contract, and\ntrigger-core `enqueueJob`; the same schema validates the payload immediately before the job handler\nruns. The queue message and enqueue/dispatch sequence do not change.\n\nThis is deliberately source-breaking for schema-less payload declarations and schema-less job\nhandlers. Keeping `.payload<T>()` or `defineJobHandler(handler)` as a silent fallback would preserve\nthe defect: the generator could recover a compile-time annotation at best, but there would be no\nruntime definition with which to prevent producer/consumer drift.\n\n## Scope and doctrine\n\n- `packages/plugin-workers-core`: Archetype 3 (Runtime/Behavior), current doctrine verdict\n  **Refactor**. The builder is a folded DSL concern, not a separate package archetype.\n- `packages/plugin-triggers-core`: Archetype 3 (Runtime/Behavior), current doctrine verdict\n  **Keep**.\n- `plugins/workers`: Archetype 5 (Plugin Package), current doctrine verdict **Refactor**. The plugin\n  remains thin: payload/schema/registry vocabulary is owned by workers-core; the plugin generator\n  only composes and emits it.\n- Scope overlays: none. This does not change an application service implementation, frontend, or\n  documentation-only surface.\n- In-scope anti-pattern checks: AP-1, AP-3, AP-8, AP-9, AP-10, AP-11, AP-13, AP-14, AP-16, AP-19,\n  AP-20, AP-22, AP-23, AP-24, and AP-25. In particular, generated `JobHandler<any>` and its blanket\n  lint suppression must disappear rather than move.\n- Relevant existing debt: `packages/plugin-workers-core` contract/domain cardinality and\n  `plugins/workers` connector split remain open but must not be deepened. The 20-diagnostic\n  `plugins/workers` private-type-ref baseline under `workers-private-type-ref-1655` is pre-existing\n  and is a strict no-increase allowance, not a pass. No new debt is planned.\n\n## Current published surface (`deno doc` at baseline)\n\nThe following facts were read from `deno doc`, not inferred from implementation files.\n\n| Entry point | Published symbol | Baseline signature relevant to #1455 |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `@netscript/plugin-workers-core` | `JobDefinition` | `JobDefinition<TId extends string = string> = Readonly<{ id; entrypoint?; name?; topic? }>` |\n| root | `JobBuilder` | `payload<TNextPayload>(): JobBuilder<..., TNextPayload, ...>`; `build(): JobDefinition<TId>` |\n| root | `defineJob` | `defineJob<TId>(id): JobBuilder<TId, \"initial\", unknown, unknown>` |\n| root | `defineJobHandler` | `defineJobHandler<TPayload = unknown>(handler): (context: JobHandlerContext<TPayload>) => JobResult<unknown> \\| Promise<...>` |\n| `./builders` | `JobDefinition` | already `JobDefinition<TId = string, TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` |\n| `./builders` | `JobBuilder` | already returns `JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>`, but `payload<T>()` is type-only |\n| `./runtime` | `JobDefinition` | already has `<TId, TPayload, TResult>` and optional `handler`, but no payload schema |\n| `./runtime` | `StaticJobRegistry` | `ReadonlyMap<string, JobHandler>` (default payload `unknown`) |\n| `./contracts/v1` | `JobTriggerInput` | non-generic `{ id?: string; payload?: Record<string, unknown>; ... }` |\n| `./contracts/v1` | `WorkersContract` | non-generic alias of the real `WorkersContractDefinition` |\n| `./contracts/v1` | `workersContract` | non-generic client-generation contract value |\n| `./contracts/v1` | `workersContractV1` / `WorkersContractV1` | non-generic context-bindable service implementer |\n| `@netscript/plugin-triggers-core` | `EnqueueJobOptions` | `EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload = unknown>`, with `payload?: TPayload` |\n| root and `./builders` | `EnqueueJobAction` | `job: JobDefinition<TJobId>` while `options` separately carries `TPayload` |\n| root and `./builders` | `enqueueJob` | `<TJobId, TPayload>(job: JobDefinition<TJobId>, options: EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>)` |\n\nThe root workers `JobDefinition` is distinct from the richer builders/domain/runtime types with the\nsame name. The root wrapper is the erasing boundary used by trigger-core today.\n\n## 1. Exact proposed public shapes\n\n### Workers payload schema and handler definition\n\nWorkers-core owns a structural Standard Schema surface; it does not re-export Zod or another\nupstream schema library.\n\n```ts\nexport type JobPayloadSchema<TPayload = unknown> = PublicStandardSchema<TPayload>;\n\nexport type JobHandler<TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown> = (\n  context: JobHandlerContext<TPayload>,\n) => JobResult<TResult> | Promise<JobResult<TResult>>;\n\nexport type JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown> =\n  & JobHandler<TPayload, TResult>\n  & Readonly<{ payloadSchema: JobPayloadSchema<TPayload> }>;\n\nexport function defineJobHandler<TPayload, TResult = unknown>(\n  payloadSchema: JobPayloadSchema<TPayload>,\n  handler: JobHandler<TPayload, TResult>,\n): JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload, TResult>;\n```\n\n`defineJobHandler` returns a callable wrapper. The callback receives the schema output type. The\nwrapper validates `context.payload` with the attached schema, replaces the callback context payload\nwith the validated output, and then invokes the callback. It throws a workers validation error on\nschema issues. This changes validation only; job selection, queueing, retry, priority, correlation,\nand handler result semantics are unchanged.\n\n### Job definition and builder\n\nThe root surface is aligned with the already-published builders/runtime generic order. Defaults keep\ntype references such as `JobDefinition<'health-check'>` valid.\n\n```ts\nexport type JobDefinition<\n  TId extends string = string,\n  TPayload = unknown,\n  TResult = unknown,\n> = Readonly<{\n  id: JobId<TId>;\n  entrypoint?: string;\n  name?: string;\n  topic?: string;\n  payloadSchema?: JobPayloadSchema<TPayload>;\n  handler?: JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload, TResult>;\n}>;\n\nexport interface JobBuilder<\n  TId extends string,\n  TConfigured extends \"initial\" | \"entrypoint-set\" | \"handler-set\",\n  TPayload,\n  TResult,\n> {\n  payload<TNextPayload>(\n    schema: JobPayloadSchema<TNextPayload>,\n  ): JobBuilder<TId, TConfigured, TNextPayload, TResult>;\n\n  handler<TNextPayload = TPayload, TNextResult = TResult>(\n    fn: JobHandler<TNextPayload, TNextResult>,\n  ): JobBuilder<TId, \"handler-set\", TNextPayload, TNextResult>;\n\n  build(): JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>; // existing typestate `this` guard retained\n}\n```\n\nThe richer `./builders` and `./runtime` `JobDefinition` shapes gain the same optional\n`payloadSchema` field. Optionality preserves manually-authored/registry-loaded legacy definitions\nthat never declared a payload. Once `.payload(schema)` is called, the builder stores that schema and\nthe built value carries it. The no-argument `.payload<T>()` overload is not retained.\n\n### Payload extraction and literal registry algebra\n\n```ts\nexport type JobPayloadOf<TDefinition> =\n  TDefinition extends JobDefinition<string, infer TPayload, unknown>\n    ? TPayload\n    : TDefinition extends JobHandlerDefinition<infer TPayload, unknown>\n      ? TPayload\n      : never;\n\nexport type JobPayloadMap<\n  TRegistry extends Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>,\n> = Readonly<{\n  [TId in keyof TRegistry]: JobPayloadOf<TRegistry[TId]>;\n}>;\n```\n\nThe workers generator emits a literal object before it widens values into runtime `Map`s:\n\n```ts\nexport const jobHandlersById = {\n  \"embed-document\": resolveJobHandler(job0, \"workers/jobs/embed-document.ts\"),\n  \"transcribe-image\": resolveJobHandler(job1, \"workers/jobs/transcribe-image.ts\"),\n} as const;\n\nexport const jobDefinitionsById = {\n  \"embed-document\": createLocalJobDefinition(\n    \"embed-document\",\n    \"./embed-document.ts\",\n    jobHandlersById[\"embed-document\"],\n  ),\n  \"transcribe-image\": createLocalJobDefinition(\n    \"transcribe-image\",\n    \"./transcribe-image.ts\",\n    jobHandlersById[\"transcribe-image\"],\n  ),\n} as const;\n\nexport type GeneratedJobDefinitionRegistry = typeof jobDefinitionsById;\nexport type GeneratedJobPayloadMap = JobPayloadMap<GeneratedJobDefinitionRegistry>;\n\n// Existing runtime exports remain Maps and retain their names.\nexport const registry: StaticJobRegistry = new Map(/* literal handler entries */);\nexport const jobDefinitions: ReadonlyMap<string, RegisterJobInput> =\n  new Map(/* serializable operational definitions */);\nexport const definitions = jobDefinitions;\n```\n\nFor that generated type:\n\n```ts\ntype GeneratedJobPayloadMap = Readonly<{\n  \"embed-document\": EmbedDocumentPayload;\n  \"transcribe-image\": TranscribeImagePayload;\n}>;\n```\n\n`createLocalJobDefinition`/`createConfiguredJobDefinition` remain responsible for the post-#1872\noperational fields. They additionally carry the handler's schema in the in-process literal\ndefinition; the existing serializable registration map keeps the existing operational projection.\nThis does not redesign metadata precedence or absorb #1451.\n\n### Typed workers `triggerJob` contract\n\n```ts\nexport type JobPayloadRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;\n\ntype JobTriggerCommon = Readonly<{\n  priority?: number;\n  delay?: number;\n  correlationId?: string;\n  traceparent?: string;\n  tracestate?: string;\n}>;\n\ntype PayloadField<TPayload> = undefined extends TPayload\n  ? Readonly<{ payload?: TPayload }>\n  : Readonly<{ payload: TPayload }>;\n\nexport type JobTriggerInput<\n  TPayloads extends JobPayloadRecord = JobPayloadRecord,\n> = string extends keyof TPayloads\n  ? Readonly<JobTriggerCommon & {\n      id?: string;\n      payload?: Record<string, unknown>;\n    }>\n  : {\n      [TId in keyof TPayloads & string]: Readonly<\n        JobTriggerCommon & { id: TId } & PayloadField<TPayloads[TId]>\n      >;\n    }[keyof TPayloads & string];\n\nexport type WorkersContract<\n  TPayloads extends JobPayloadRecord = JobPayloadRecord,\n> = WorkersContractDefinition<TPayloads>;\n\nexport function createWorkersContract<\n  TPayloads extends JobPayloadRecord = JobPayloadRecord,\n>(): WorkersContract<TPayloads>;\n```\n\n`WorkersContractDefinition<TPayloads>` remains the real oRPC contract type; only its `triggerJob`\nroute input schema type is parameterized. `createWorkersContract()` returns the same runtime\ncontract definition as `workersContract`, with the generic narrowing proven safe because every map\nvalue is constrained to the route's existing record payload domain. This is the canonical published\nsurface, not an EIS-Chat compatibility shim.\n\nThe existing `workersContract`, `workersContractV1`, and `WorkersContractV1` remain the broad default\nservice implementation surfaces. Services must implement the whole runtime route, while a client\nopts into its application registry:\n\n```ts\nconst contract = createWorkersContract<GeneratedJobPayloadMap>();\nconst client = createServiceClient({ contract, serviceName: \"workers\" });\n\nawait client.triggerJob({\n  id: \"embed-document\",\n  payload: { documentId: \"doc-1\", text: \"...\" },\n});\n\nawait client.triggerJob({\n  id: \"transcribe-image\",\n  // @ts-expect-error EmbedDocumentPayload is not TranscribeImagePayload.\n  payload: { documentId: \"doc-1\", text: \"...\" },\n});\n```\n\n### Trigger-core enqueue binding\n\n```ts\nexport type EnqueueJobAction<\n  TJobId extends string = string,\n  TPayload = unknown,\n> = Readonly<{\n  kind: \"enqueue-job\";\n  job: JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>;\n  jobId: JobId<TJobId>;\n  options: EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>;\n}>;\n\nexport function enqueueJob<TJobId extends string, TPayload = unknown>(\n  job: JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>,\n  options: EnqueueJobOptions<NoInfer<TPayload>>,\n): EnqueueJobAction<TJobId, TPayload>;\n```\n\n`NoInfer` is essential. Without it TypeScript may infer or widen `TPayload` from both arguments,\nrecreating the independent-payload defect as a union/escape. The selected definition is the only\ninference source; the options payload is checked against it.\n\n## 2. Published signature changes and source compatibility\n\n| Published symbol | Proposed change | Source-breaking? |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| root `JobDefinition` | Add `TPayload = unknown`, `TResult = unknown`, optional schema and handler fields | **No for type references** because defaults preserve `JobDefinition` and `JobDefinition<TId>`; optional fields preserve structural values. Code depending on the old type having exactly one generic parameter only through a higher-order generic alias may need adjustment, but normal uses remain valid. |\n| root `JobBuilder.payload` | Replace `.payload<T>()` with `.payload(schema)` and infer `T` from the schema | **Yes.** Every schema-less call must supply its runtime schema. This is required to close drift. |\n| root `JobBuilder.build` | Return `JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>` instead of `JobDefinition<TId>` | **No for correct consumers; intentional tightening downstream.** Payload/result information is additive until a caller passes an incompatible payload. |\n| root `defineJobHandler` | Require `(payloadSchema, handler)` and return `JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload, TResult>` | **Yes.** One-argument handler declarations must pass their existing schema (or introduce one). This is how generation recovers both type and validator. |\n| new root symbols `JobPayloadSchema`, `JobHandler`, `JobHandlerDefinition`, `JobPayloadOf`, `JobPayloadMap` | Add contract algebra | No; additive. |\n| `./builders` `JobDefinition` | Add optional `payloadSchema` to the underlying domain shape | No; additive optional field with existing generic defaults/order. |\n| `./builders` `JobBuilder.payload` | Require schema argument | **Yes**, same migration as root. |\n| `./runtime` `JobDefinition` | Add optional `payloadSchema` | No; additive optional field. |\n| `./runtime` `StaticJobRegistry` | Use a payload-erased existential handler (`JobHandler<never, unknown>`) instead of `JobHandler<unknown, unknown>` so heterogeneous typed handlers can be stored without `any` | **Potentially yes.** Code that reads a static handler and invokes it without first correlating it with a definition will stop compiling. Construction from specifically typed handlers becomes valid. Runtime dispatch already performs the correlation/cast at the registry boundary. |\n| `./contracts/v1` `JobTriggerInput` | Add payload-map generic and a literal-id discriminated union; retain broad default branch | No for existing `JobTriggerInput` uses because the default reproduces the old optional `id`/record payload shape. **Opted-in typed uses intentionally reject** missing ids and mismatched payloads. |\n| `./contracts/v1` `WorkersContract` | Add payload-map generic with broad default | No for existing uses; default preserves current type. |\n| new `./contracts/v1` `createWorkersContract` | Add canonical typed client-contract factory | No; additive. |\n| `workersContract`, `workersContractV1`, `WorkersContractV1` | Keep broad default signatures | No change. The service implementer is not application-registry-specific. |\n| trigger-core `EnqueueJobAction` | Carry payload generic into its `job` field | No for existing references because generic defaults remain. |\n| trigger-core `enqueueJob` | Infer payload from `JobDefinition<TId, TPayload>` and apply `NoInfer` to options | **Yes, intentionally, for incorrect calls.** A payload belonging to another definition stops compiling. Correct calls and untyped legacy `JobDefinition<TId>` calls retain behavior (`TPayload = unknown`). Explicit generic calls whose declared payload disagrees with the definition also stop compiling. |\n| generated module exports | Add literal `jobHandlersById`, `jobDefinitionsById`, and generated type aliases; preserve `registry`, `jobDefinitions`, and `definitions` Map exports | No for supported exported names. Consumers parsing the generated source text or relying on the non-public internal `jobHandlers` array must update. |\n| generated job source convention | Require every `defineJobHandler` call to supply a Standard Schema | **Yes.** Existing handler modules must pass the schema they commonly already use manually. Schema-less arbitrary function exports cannot provide the promised id→payload contract and are rejected by generation with a path-specific error. |\n\n## 3. Binding rules\n\n### Workers service `triggerJob`\n\nThe generated `GeneratedJobPayloadMap` is passed to `createWorkersContract`. The generic contract's\n`triggerJob` input is a discriminated union keyed by literal `id`; each union member's `payload` is\nthe mapped payload for only that id. The broad `workersContractV1` service implementer remains\nunchanged and receives the same wire object. No router selection, HTTP path, queue, or persistence\nlogic changes.\n\n### Trigger-core `enqueueJob`\n\nThe function infers `TId` and `TPayload` exclusively from the selected `JobDefinition`. `NoInfer` on\n`EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>` turns the second argument into a checking position. The action retains\nboth generic arguments, so later trigger processors cannot lose the correlation internally.\n\n## 4. Generated registration boundary\n\nToday the manifest forces `mapValueType: \"JobHandler<any>\"`, adds a file-wide\n`no-explicit-any` suppression, puts handlers into a homogeneous array, and only then builds the\nregistry. That is the exact erasure point.\n\nThe generator will instead:\n\n1. import each job module as it does now;\n2. resolve each module with a generic `resolveJobHandler<TModule>(module, path)` whose conditional\n   return type preserves the concrete default/named handler type;\n3. emit `jobHandlersById` as a literal `as const` object, before any `Map` widening;\n4. emit `jobDefinitionsById` from those exact handler values, preserving each attached\n   `payloadSchema` type while applying the existing #1451 operational policy projection;\n5. derive `GeneratedJobPayloadMap` with workers-core `JobPayloadMap`;\n6. project the literal objects into the existing runtime `registry`, `jobDefinitions`, and\n   `definitions` maps;\n7. remove `mapValueType: \"JobHandler<any>\"` and the generated lint suppression.\n\nBoth workers registry emitters (`runtime-registry-generator.ts` and the package-owned\n`registry-compiler.ts`) must emit the same contract. Their golden/semantic tests will assert literal\nkeys, absence of `any`/lint suppression, preservation of existing operational definition fields,\nand compilation of the generated module.\n\n## 5. One definition for runtime validation\n\nThe Standard Schema passed to `.payload(schema)` or `defineJobHandler(schema, handler)` owns all\nthree views:\n\n1. schema output infers the handler callback payload;\n2. generated `JobPayloadMap` exposes that same output to producers (`triggerJob` and `enqueueJob`);\n3. the returned/built handler definition carries that schema and validates the unknown wire payload\n   immediately before the application handler runs.\n\nThe generator never parses TypeScript syntax and never asks an application to duplicate a payload\ninterface. It only transports the typed handler/definition value. A Zod schema, Valibot schema, or\nother Standard Schema implementation works through the package-owned structural contract.\n\nRuntime validation happens at the handler boundary rather than rewriting the enqueue route. An\ninvalid untyped producer can still place the same message on the queue, but the consumer rejects it\nbefore application code. Typed producers cannot construct the mismatched `triggerJob`/`enqueueJob`\ncall in the first place. This preserves enqueue ordering, delay, priority, correlation, idempotency,\nand retry behavior.\n\n## 6. Defaults and what cannot be preserved\n\nDefaults:\n\n- `JobDefinition<TId = string, TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` preserves zero- and one-\n  argument references.\n- `JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` and payload helper types default to\n  `unknown` for generic library code.\n- `EnqueueJobAction<TId = string, TPayload = unknown>` preserves existing references.\n- `JobTriggerInput<JobPayloadRecord>` and `WorkersContract<JobPayloadRecord>` reproduce the current\n  broad service/client surface when no application map is supplied.\n- Legacy manually-created `JobDefinition<TId>` values remain structurally valid because\n  `payloadSchema` and `handler` are optional. They remain untyped (`unknown`) and therefore cannot\n  claim the new invariant.\n\nCannot be preserved:\n\n- Schema-less `.payload<T>()`: a type argument disappears at runtime, so retaining it would violate\n  the single-definition requirement.\n- One-argument `defineJobHandler(handler)`: without an attached schema the generator cannot produce\n  runtime validation from the same definition.\n- Arbitrary generated job modules found only by “first function export”: that heuristic erases the\n  handler contract. Generated jobs must export a schema-backed handler as `default` or `handler`.\n- Mismatched payload calls: rejecting them is the acceptance criterion and an intentional source\n  break.\n- Invoking a value read directly from heterogeneous `StaticJobRegistry` without a correlated job\n  definition: the existential registry type makes that unsafe operation uncallable.\n- Typed `triggerJob` calls that omit the literal id because an HTTP path will add it later: the id is\n  the discriminant at a TypeScript call site and must be present. The broad service implementer\n  still accepts the path-populated shape.\n- Consumers that inspect exact generated source text or private intermediate arrays: generated code\n  is rewritten to preserve literal types. Stable runtime export names remain.\n\n## RED → GREEN proof\n\nThe RED commit adds a consumer-site type test using two real schema-backed definitions:\n\n```ts\ntype EmbedDocumentPayload = { documentId: string; text: string };\ntype TranscribeImagePayload = { imageUrl: string; language?: string };\n\nconst embedDocument = defineJob(\"embed-document\")\n  .payload(EmbedDocumentPayloadSchema)\n  .handler(/* correctly typed handler */)\n  .build();\n\nconst transcribeImage = defineJob(\"transcribe-image\")\n  .payload(TranscribeImagePayloadSchema)\n  .handler(/* correctly typed handler */)\n  .build();\n\nenqueueJob(embedDocument, { payload: embedPayload }); // positive control\nenqueueJob(transcribeImage, {\n  // @ts-expect-error EmbedDocumentPayload does not belong to transcribe-image.\n  payload: embedPayload,\n});\n```\n\nAt RED, the check fails with “Unused `@ts-expect-error` directive”, proving current main accepts the\nwrong payload. It must not fail because of a missing import, invalid job definition, or unrelated\nschema error. GREEN is the same fixture compiling with the directive consumed. A second fixture\nchecks `createWorkersContract<GeneratedJobPayloadMap>().triggerJob` with the same pair. Both commit\nSHAs are recorded in `worklog.md`.\n\n## Commit slices\n\n1. **Plan contract** — this file only. Gate: human/PLAN-EVAL review. Files: `plan.md`.\n2. **RED consumer proof** — add positive and negative compile-time fixtures without changing source.\n   Gate: targeted check fails only on unused `@ts-expect-error`. Files: workers/trigger-core type\n   test(s), `worklog.md`, `context-pack.md`.\n3. **Core type carrier** — schema-backed handler/definition/builder shapes, payload map algebra,\n   typed workers contract, and enqueue binding. Gate: targeted package checks/tests turn RED green.\n   Files under `packages/plugin-workers-core`, `packages/plugin-triggers-core`, plus run artifacts.\n4. **Generated application boundary** — both registry emitters, manifest, scaffold/built-in job\n   declarations, golden and semantic generated-code tests. Gate: plugin package tests and generated\n   module check. Files under `plugins/workers`, plus run artifacts.\n5. **Merge-readiness gates and evidence** — no product changes unless a gate finds an in-scope\n   defect. Gate set below; update only run artifacts/PR evidence.\n\n## Gate set\n\n- Scoped `run-deno-check.ts --root <root> --ext ts,tsx` for each touched root, with `--unstable-kv`\n  supplied through the wrapper/toolchain where applicable.\n- Each touched package/plugin test suite through `run-deno-test.ts` or its package task.\n- Root `deno task check` and `deno task test`, because published cross-package types change.\n- Scoped `run-deno-lint.ts` and `run-deno-fmt.ts` for each touched root.\n- `deno task quality:scan` / `deno task quality:gate` (framework-source requirement).\n- Full-export `deno task doc:lint` for touched published units. The workers plugin's recorded 20\n  private-type-ref diagnostics must not increase or gain another diagnostic class.\n- JSR package audit for every touched published unit.\n- `deno task publish:dry-run`.\n- `deno task arch:check`.\n- Consumer compilation of generated registry plus triggerJob/enqueueJob type fixtures.\n- Runtime tests proving schema validation occurs before handler application without changing\n  enqueue message construction or action shape.\n\nThe full `scaffold.runtime` E2E is not selected initially: this plan changes generated TypeScript but\nnot scaffold installation, DB wiring, Aspire helper generation, or runtime resource topology. If\nthe generator tests cannot prove the emitted module in a real scaffold, that is a rescope trigger\nand the canonical one-pass suite becomes required.\n\n## Risk register\n\n| Risk | Mitigation |\n| --- | --- |\n| TypeScript infers payload from both `job` and `options`, allowing a union | Put `NoInfer` on the options position and lock it with the wrong-job compile fixture. |\n| Contract typing becomes another hand-authored oRPC shim | Parameterize the existing real `WorkersContractDefinition`; keep runtime schema/value identical and run contract soundness tests. |\n| Standard Schema inference exposes upstream types or slow types | Publish a workers-owned structural alias, run full doc-lint and publish dry-run. |\n| Generated literal typing is widened before extraction | Emit `as const` objects first; derive aliases before projecting to `Map`. Compile the emitted module. |\n| `any` moves from manifest to a cast/helper | Remove the preamble and run `quality:scan`; no `any`, blanket lint ignore, or `as unknown as` is allowed. |\n| Runtime validation accidentally changes enqueue/retry behavior | Validate only inside the schema-backed handler wrapper and compare action/message construction tests. |\n| #1451 operational registry metadata is regressed | Preserve `RegisterJobInput` projection and policy-precedence golden assertions; do not redesign generated operational fields. |\n| Existing worker modules break without a usable migration | Update first-party/stub/sample modules in the same slice and document the mechanical migration: pass the schema already used for parsing to `defineJobHandler`. |\n| Pre-existing workers doc-lint debt hides a regression | Record baseline diagnostic count/class separately and require no increase; all other touched units must be zero. |\n\n## Open-decision sweep\n\n- **Resolved now:** schema-less payload APIs are source-breaking and removed. Deferring this would\n  force generator and runtime rewrites later.\n- **Resolved now:** runtime validation occurs at the handler boundary; enqueue semantics remain\n  unchanged.\n- **Resolved now:** the broad service implementer stays broad; application clients opt into the\n  literal map with `createWorkersContract<...>()`.\n- **Resolved now:** generated runtime Map export names remain stable; new literal objects/type aliases\n  are additive.\n- **Safe to defer:** a future input/output split for Standard Schema defaults/transforms. This issue\n  maps schema output as the payload contract; supporting distinct producer input and handler output\n  types can add an additional generic later without changing this id correlation.\n- **Safe to defer:** task/workflow payload parity. #1455 is job-specific; copying the change to task\n  or workflow builders without a named consumer invariant would expand scope.\n\nNo must-resolve decision remains open before implementation.\n\n## Explicit non-goals\n\n- #1451 operational metadata design or any alternate job discovery/loading system.\n- A temporary EIS-Chat/shared-contract adapter or other consumer-side compatibility shim.\n- Rewriting queueing, scheduling, retries, idempotency, correlation, service routing, or worker\n  dispatch.\n- Task/workflow payload mapping.\n- Closing unrelated doctrine debt or the workers private-type-ref issue.\n\n## Contributor path\n\nA contributor adds a job by defining one Standard Schema next to the handler, passing it to\n`defineJobHandler(schema, handler)`, and exporting that definition as `default` or `handler`.\nRunning the existing workers registry generator emits the literal id entry, payload-map alias,\nruntime handler Map, and operational definition Map. Producers import/re-export the generated\n`GeneratedJobPayloadMap` and instantiate `createWorkersContract<GeneratedJobPayloadMap>()`; trigger\nbuilders pass the built `JobDefinition` directly to `enqueueJob`.\n","numLines":518,"startLine":1,"totalLines":518}}}

Wait — `NoInfer` is TS 5.4+. Deno uses 5.x TS — fine. TPayload is inferred from the job arg only, options is checked position. But the job arg is `JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>` where payloadSchema/handler are optional: inferring TPayload from a legacy definition without schema → probably unknown. For a typed definition, inference across both payloadSchema and handler fields could yield a candidate; should settle on P. Consistent. Does it prevent the defect? Yes: embedDocument's payload passed to transcribeImage's definition — TPayload=TranscribeImagePayload is inferred from the job, options is checked against it → error. Good.\n\nJobTriggerInput union — id must be present (`id: TId` required in typed branch). Payload per id. Prevents defect at typed client. Good.\n\nOne subtlety: `EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload = unknown>` has `payload?: TPayload`. For a required payload P, options.payload?: P — omitting payload is still allowed! PayloadField handles this in the trigger contract (undefined extends TPayload), but the enqueue path's EnqueueJobOptions keeps `payload?: TPayload`. Omitting payload for a job that requires it compiles (undefined accepted). Does that break the invariant? The invariant is \"a payload for one job can't compile if enqueued on another\" — it doesn't cover omission. The plan doesn't claim to cover omission in enqueueJob. Hmm, but \"binding the payload of triggerJob and trigger-core's enqueueJob to the chosen job definition/ID\" (issue's expectation). Omission: `enqueueJob(embedDocument, {})` — compiles because payload is optional. Is this a hole in the claimed invariant? The issue's invariant is about wrong payload; plan's §Decision states the invariant is id↔payload. Not required. Non-blocking note.\n\nActually wait — does `undefined extends TPayload` handle this? For P = EmbedDocumentPayload (an object), undefined doesn't extend P so in the *trigger contract*, PayloadField requires payload. But enqueueJob's `EnqueueJobOptions<P>` keeps baseline `payload?: P`. The plan doesn't change EnqueueJobOptions's requiredness. Inconsistency between two paths (triggerJob requires, enqueueJob allows omission) — worth noting, but not claimed.\n\n4. **Generated registration** — §4 removes mapValueType `JobHandler<any>` and the blanket suppression, step 7 says remove. StaticJobRegistry gets an existential `JobHandler<never, unknown>` — wait, `JobHandler<never, unknown>`: parameters JobHandlerContext<never>, contravariance allows storing a handler for any payload P (JobHandlerContext<P>… assigning JobHandler<P> to JobHandler<never> requires JobHandlerContext<never> → JobHandlerContext<P>: never's payload is assignable to P — yes never is assignable to anything). But JobHandlerContext<never> — the handler can't be invoked without first correlating; calling with a real context of JobHandlerContext<P>… to call JobHandler<never> you'd need JobHandlerContext<never>, which you can't produce. So calling a registry-read is uncompilable without correlation — matches the plan's \"uncallable\" claim. Good, consistent.\n\nDoes the plan eliminate any at the application boundary rather than relocating it? resolveJobHandler<TModule> is generic and preserves types. Risk register addresses \"any moves from manifest to casts/helpers\" by banning via quality:scan. OK.\n\n5. **Runtime validation** — same schema validates at handler boundary; queue messages and enqueue/dispatch sequence unchanged. Handler wrapper validates context.payload before calling user handler. Consistent with claim. \"Producers and consumers cannot drift\" — single schema source. Caveat: legacy schema-less definitions (optional payloadSchema) validate nothing — plan admits \"they remain untyped (unknown) and therefore cannot claim the new invariant.\" Good, honest.\n\n6. **Default-type-argument strategy** — §6 enumerates defaults and what cannot be preserved. Consistent with §1/§2.\n\nNow, verifying baseline claims against actual code. The plan's credibility depends on the recorded deno doc baseline. I should spot-check a few lines with deno doc or source reads:\n\n- root's JobDefinition (packages/plugin-workers-core/mod.ts?)\n- root's JobBuilder.payload and build\n- root's defineJobHandler\n- ./contracts/v1's JobTriggerInput\n- ./runtime's StaticJobRegistry\n- triggers-core's enqueueJob\n\nAlso verify the generated `JobHandler<any>` + blanket lint suppression claim (manifest mapValueType).\n\nLet me run deno doc commands. Time budget ~10 min; let's be quick. Use `deno doc` against local package entry points.\n\nI need the package paths: packages/plugin-workers-core and packages/plugin-triggers-core. Entry points: mod.ts probably exports root; ./builders, ./runtime, ./contracts/v1 subpaths from deno.json exports.\n\nLet me do it in a batch.\n\nLoaded the plan (since the docs-only commit after `f655c3405`, `plan.md` hasn't been changed). never\n      : JobBuilder<TId, TConfigured, TPayload, TResult>,\n  ): JobBuilder<TId, TConfigured, TNextPayload, TResult>;\n  /** Set the cron schedule expression for this job.\n   * @deprecated Define recurring work with `defineScheduledTrigger(...).enqueueJob(...)`.\n   */\n  schedule(expression: string): this;\n  /** Set the schedule timezone. */\n  timezone(value: string): this;\n  /** Set the execution timeout in milliseconds. */\n  timeout(ms: number): this;\n  /** Set retry count and optional retry behavior. */\n  retry(maxRetries: number, options?: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): this;\n  /** Set execution permissions for this job. */\n  permissions(perms: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): this;\n  /** Add tags to this job definition. */\n  tags(...tags: string[]): this;\n  /** Merge metadata into this job definition. */\n  metadata(data: Record<string, unknown>): this;\n  /** Set execution retention policy for this job. */\n  retention(options: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): this;\n  /** Enable or disable this job definition. */\n  enabled(value: boolean): this;\n  /** Set the worker topic for this job. */\n  topic(name: string): this;\n  /** Set the queue trigger name for this job. */\n  queueTrigger(name: string): this;\n  /** Build the job definition after an entrypoint or handler has been configured. */\n  build(\n    this: TConfigured extends 'entrypoint-set' | 'handler-set'\n      ?
