{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"gen-1788365657-CmcDypmU9Qd4s2dqXNY9","type":"message","role":"assistant","container":null,"content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Verified all the major baseline claims:\n\n1. The manifest enforces `mapValueType: \"JobHandler<any>\"` and `preamble: [\"// deno-lint-ignore-file no-explicit-any\"]` — §4's claim is accurate. ✓\n2. Root JobDefinition, JobBuilder.payload type-only, defineJob, defineJobHandler — matches the plan's table. ✓\n3. ./contracts/v1's JobTriggerInput is non-generic, the broad option `id?`; WorkersContract alias is non-generic; workersContract/workersContractV1 are non-generic. ✓\n4. ./runtime's StaticJobRegistry = ReadonlyMap<string, JobHandler> (defaults to unknown payload); runtime's JobDefinition is already <TId,TPayload,TResult> with optional handler. ✓\n5. triggers-core's enqueueJob/EnqueueJobAction/EnqueueJobOptions matches the table exactly — `job: JobDefinition<TJobId>` and `options: EnqueueJobOptions<TPayload>` are inferred independently. ✓\n\nNote: The plan says the root JobBuilder's `handler` takes a `JobHandler<TNextPayload, TNextResult>` — the baseline root's handler parameter is an inline `(context: Readonly<{id; payload}>) => ...`; the proposed JobHandler is the type of the ./runtime module. builders' `JobHandler`/`JobHandlerContext` also exist. Fine point: root currently doesn't export the `JobHandler` type; the plan proposes a new root export JobHandler — it's included in §2's new symbols line (\"new root symbols ... JobHandler\" — yes, `JobHandler` is listed). Good.\n\nNext, evaluate the six decisions.\n\n**D1. Public shape.** The shape is consistent. Verification of key inference concerns:\n\n- `JobPayloadOf` against `JobDefinition<string, infer P, unknown>`: the definition type is `JobDefinition<\"embed-document\", EmbedDocumentPayload, R>`. Assignability check against `JobDefinition<string, P, unknown>` — id literal → string is OK; payloadSchema?: JobPayloadSchema<P> infers P=EmbedDocumentPayload from structural inference against the `~standard.types.output` field, and also from handler?: JobHandlerDefinition<P,R>… multiple inference candidates: P appears in payloadSchema (output position) and in handler's parameter (contravariant position). TS collects candidates from inference sites; contravariant sites are preferred when candidates conflict, but here both sites yield the same EmbedDocumentPayload so they converge. But — is there an ordering issue: is a typed definition actually *assignable* to `JobDefinition<string, EmbedDocumentPayload, unknown>` in the first place? TResult position: `unknown` — handler?: JobHandlerDefinition<P, R> against JobHandlerDefinition<P, unknown> → JobHandler<P,R>'s return JobResult<R> vs JobResult<unknown> — covariant, R assignable to unknown OK. So the match succeeds and extracts P. For a legacy schema-less `JobDefinition<TId>` = <TId, unknown, unknown> → P = unknown. Consistent.\n\n- One subtle risk: the `as const` on the generated literal `jobDefinitionsById`: `createLocalJobDefinition(\"embed-document\", \"./embed-document.ts\", jobHandlersById[\"embed-document\"])` needs to return a type where the payload is inferrable — `createLocalJobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>(id, entrypoint, handler: JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload,TResult>): JobDefinition<TId, TPayload, TResult>`. The plan doesn't spell out that signature, but §4 step 4 says \"emit jobDefinitionsById from those exact handler values, preserving each attached payloadSchema type\". Implied generic pass-through. OK at plan granularity.\n\n- `JobPayloadMap` constraint `TRegistry extends Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`: typeof jobDefinitionsById is an anonymous mapped/`as const` object → satisfies the implicit index signature. OK. And the resulting payload EmbedDocumentPayload needs to be assignable to unknown for JobPayloadRecord: no issue.\n\n- The invariant is expressible: GeneratedJobPayloadMap produces exactly `{readonly \"embed-document\": EmbedDocumentPayload; \"transcribe-image\": ...}`, used both by JobTriggerInput's union and enqueueJob. Sufficient. PASS.\n\n**D2. Breaking-changes surface.** The table matches the verified baseline. Comprehensiveness check for under-stated breaks:\n\n- §2's root JobBuilder.payload says Yes ✓, defineJobHandler Yes ✓, builders payload Yes ✓, enqueueJob Yes (for wrong invocations) ✓, generated job source convention Yes ✓, StaticJobRegistry \"potentially yes\" ✓.\n- What about the *root JobBuilder.handler*'s parameter change from inline context to JobHandler (JobHandlerContext)? JobHandlerContext<TPayload,TResult> = DomainJobContext<...> presumably has more fields; handler functions declared with a narrower parameter are still accepted (contravariance). Existing `.handler((ctx) => ...)` with an inline-typed ctx of `{id: string; payload: T}` — assigning that function to a parameter of type JobHandler needs JobHandlerContext to be assignable to {id,payload} — yes. No break. OK.\n- Root's `defineJobHandler` return shape: from plain function to `JobHandlerDefinition` (function & {payloadSchema}) — callable, so return consumers are unaffected; widening the return is not a break.\n- The root JobDefinition adding optional `handler?: JobHandlerDefinition<TPayload,TResult>`: optional property addition — for *values* structurally assigned to JobDefinition, fine; but the contravariant parameter issue: `handler?: (c: JobHandlerContext<P>) => ...` vs old JobDefinition had no handler — the addition doesn't break old code. What could break: someone passing `JobDefinition<'a', P, R>` where `JobDefinition<'a'>` (unknown payload) is expected — typed → unknown assignment fails in the handler field (contravariant parameter). §2's row about build()'s \"No for correct consumers; intentional tightening downstream\" partially covers this. This is a variance edge case; the plan acknowledges tightening downstream. I think acceptable; mention in non-blocking note? Actually, wait: does `JobDefinition<'x', P>` assign to `JobDefinition<'x'>` = `JobDefinition<'x', unknown, unknown>`? handler?: JobHandler<P,R> vs JobHandler<unknown,unknown>: parameter JobHandlerContext<unknown> vs JobHandlerContext<P> — strictFunctionTypes applies to function type properties (not methods): for `handler?: JobHandler<P>` assignable to `handler?: JobHandler<unknown>`, we'd need JobHandler<P> → JobHandler<unknown> i.e. (c: HC<P>)=>r assignable to (c: HC<unknown>)=>r requires HC<unknown> → HC<P>: unknown→P fails. So NO, a definition with a typed *handler* can't widen to unknown. But a definition built with `.payload(schema).handler(fn)` — is fn stored? JobHandlerDefinition has a payloadSchema. So consumers annotating `JobDefinition<'x'>` for typed jobs will break. This is precisely what \"intentional tightening downstream\" says. OK, not under-stated.\n- The `payload()` overload removal covered as Yes.\n- One more: proposed §1's JobBuilder drops the `this` guard on `payload` (typestate \"cannot call payload after handler-set\"). The current baseline root `payload` has a `this:` guard rejecting after handler-set. The plan's interface shows payload without a this-guard and only build() retains the guard (by comment). Dropping payload's guard is a *relaxation* — not source-breaking, but changes the established runtime behavior? If at runtime the guard still rejects… the plan's §1 code as written doesn't include the guard. Minor consistency gap, not a contract break. Non-blocking note.\n\nConclusion: the surface is comprehensive and accurate against the verified baseline. PASS.\n\n**D3. Trigger binding.** enqueueJob: `job: JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>` + `options: EnqueueJobOptions<NoInfer<TPayload>>` — TPayload inferred only from job; options checked. Mismatched payload → compile error. NoInfer exists in TS ≥5.4 (repo is Deno 2.9 → TS 5.7+). Prevents defect. ✓\n\nJobTriggerInput: literal-ids discriminated union; `id: \"transcribe-image\"` requires payload TranscribeImagePayload; embed's payload fails. Note: excess property check — passing `{documentId, text}` to a TranscribeImagePayload member will error anyway; and structural mismatch. But subtle: if EmbedDocumentPayload happened to be structurally assignable to TranscribeImagePayload, the wrong-payload pass would compile — inherent to structural typing; the issue's example has disjoint fields. Acceptable; not the plan's defect. Also `PayloadField` makes payload required when TPayload doesn't include undefined — good for the trigger path. The EnqueueJobOptions asymmetry (payload remains optional even for required payloads, so `enqueueJob(embedDocument, {})` compiles) — this is a real gap against \"bind the enqueueJob payload to the definition\", but the *stated* defect (wrong payload for another job) is prevented; omission is not in the invariant. Non-blocking note.\n\nAlso EnqueueJobAction carries `<TJobId, TPayload>` into the job field `JobDefinition<TJobId, TPayload>` — preserves correlation for later processors ✓.\n\nPASS.\n\n**D4. Generated registration.** §4 verified against reality: manifest enforces mapValueType JobHandler<any> + blanket preamble; plan removes both, emits literal `as const` objects before Map widening, generic resolveJobHandler preserves the concrete type, StaticJobRegistry uses a JobHandler<never> existential — but wait: the registry is `ReadonlyMap<string, JobHandler>` — changing the value type of StaticJobRegistry to JobHandler<never, unknown>… the *generated* `registry: StaticJobRegistry = new Map(literal handler entries)` — a typed handler JobHandler<P> assignable to JobHandler<never>? Needs HC<never> → HC<P>: payload never assignable to P ✓ (never → anything). OK, heterogeneous storage works. Call is uncallable ✓ as claimed. And quality:scan guards against `any` relocation; the risk register addresses cast relocation. Also the golden tests assert absence of `any`/suppression. Eliminates, not relocates, at plan level. PASS.\n\nOne question: §4 mentions \"package-owned `registry-compiler.ts`\" — verified it exists and currently uses a homogeneous-array-with-cast `StaticJobHandler`. The plan requires both emitters to produce the same contract. ✓.\n\n**D5. Runtime validation.** A single schema flows through three views; validation inside a defineJobHandler wrapper immediately before the handler executes; queue messages and enqueue/dispatch sequences are unchanged (envelope not modified, validation wraps the callback). The plan is honest: untyped producers can still enqueue bad messages; the consumer rejects before the app code — that's what's stated. \"Producer and consumer can't drift\" — via the generated map and the handler wrapper, both derive from the same schema object. But schema-less legacy definitions skip validation — the plan acknowledges that in §6. Also the builder path: does build()'s wrapper validate? §1 describes defineJobHandler wrapper validation; builder `.payload(schema).handler(fn)` — \"the builder stores that schema and the built value carries it\"; runtime dispatch presumably needs to use definition.payloadSchema/handler. The plan says validation happens \"immediately before the job handler runs\" via \"returned/built handler definition carrying that schema and validating\". For builder-attached handlers, the handler dispatch must consult the schema — an implementation detail left slightly implicit (the root JobBuilder.handler wraps fn? unclear). §1 says the wrapper validates against the attached schema for defineJobHandler; for builder-set handlers, the built JobDefinition carries payloadSchema + handler, and runtime dispatch \"already performs correlation/casting at the registry boundary\" (line in §2). The gate set includes \"a runtime test proving schema validation happens before handler application without changing the enqueue message construction or action shape\". Acceptable at plan level. PASS.\n\nHmm — one sharp point: for `.entrypoint()`-based jobs (module file handlers via defineJobHandler) validation is in the handler wrapper. For in-process `.handler(fn)` on a builder with payload schema — who wraps? If the dispatch calls handler directly without consulting definition.payloadSchema, the \"same schema validates\" claim doesn't hold on that path. Is that a FAIL under the rule \"unable to achieve the stated invariant\"? Decision 5's claim: same schema drives runtime validation so producer/consumer can't drift \"without changing queue messages or the enqueue/dispatch sequences\". The defineJobHandler wrapper mechanism is explicit; the builder path is under-specified. But the gate forces a runtime test proving validation-before-handler without changing action shape. Since the decision is \"schema owned by consumer; validated at handler boundary,\" and both carrier paths (builder definition and JobHandlerDefinition) are typed to carry the schema, I'll call it a non-blocking precision note rather than a contradiction or inability. The builder handler path can wrap fn inside build() with payloadSchema — no queue/dispatch sequence change. PASS with note.\n\n**D6. Default strategy.** JobDefinition<TId, TPayload=unknown, TResult=unknown> defaults preserve `JobDefinition` and `JobDefinition<TId>` — verified against baseline root (single-parameter). EnqueueJobAction defaults ✓ (baseline shows `<TJobId extends string = string, TPayload = unknown>` — the plan matches). JobTriggerInput/WorkersContract default broad branch reproduces the current shape (string extends keyof JobPayloadRecord → loose branch with id?, payload?: Record<string,unknown>) ✓ — verified that `string extends keyof Record<string,unknown>` is true. \"What can't be preserved\" list: comprehensive — schema-less payload, one-arg defineJobHandler, first-function-export heuristic, mismatched calls, direct registry calls, id omission, source-text consumers. PASS.\n\nOne more consistency check on D1/D6: the proposed root JobDefinition's `TId extends string = string` — baseline was `TId extends string = string` ✓ same constraint. builders' baseline `JobDefinition<TId = string, ...>` (per plan's table, builders' has `<TId = string, TPayload = unknown, TResult = unknown>` — unverified but plausible; minor).\n\nAlso the plan's `JobPayloadOf` handles JobHandlerDefinition secondarily — for jobHandlersById, JobPayloadMap over the definitions is used; handlersById map is also extractable. Plan says \"throws workers validation error on schema problems\" — presented as validation-only change. Fine.\n2. `createWorkersContract()` \"generic narrowing is safe because every map value is constrained to the route's existing record-payload domain, thus proven\" — \"proven safe\" is a strong word: type-level constraint doesn't prove runtime wire compatibility of a narrowed client against the broad server schema — but the narrowing is a compile-time restriction of what clients may send; the route's runtime schema still accepts a record. It's plausible; \"proven\" is aspirational and the gate includes \"contract sanity tests\". Worth a non-blocking mention.\n3. The RED→GREEN fixture is correctly designed (fails at RED only via unused `@ts-expect-error`).\n\n4. **Generated registration — PASS.** §4 removes `mapValueType: \"JobHandler<any>\"` and the preamble outright (step 7), derives payload types from `as const` literals before any Map widening, and the risk register plus `quality:scan` gate explicitly forbid relocating `any`/`as unknown as` into the helper. The `JobHandler<never, unknown>` registry value is genuinely uncallable-without-correlation as claimed (never→any payload succeeds; real contexts don't), so erasure is eliminated, not moved. Both named emitters are covered.\n\n5. **Runtime validation — PASS.** One schema owning inference, producer map, and the handler-boundary validator cannot drift by construction; the wrapper validates the wire payload immediately before application code without touching message construction or the enqueue/dispatch sequence, and §5 is honest that an untyped producer can still enqueue (rejected at consumption, not submission) — the claim is stated at exactly its evidence level.\n\n6. **Default-type-argument strategy — PASS.** Defaults preserve zero/one-arg `JobDefinition`, `EnqueueJobAction`, and the broad contract surface (all verified against baseline defaults); the \"cannot be preserved\" list is complete and each entry is a genuine consequence of the invariant, correctly framed as deliberate trade-off rather than oversight.\n\n## Non-blocking observations (verdict unaffected)\n\n- **§1 payload `this` guard dropped in the shown interface.** The baseline root `.payload<T>()` carries `this: TConfigured extends 'handler-set' ? never : …`, but the proposed `payload(schema)` signature shows no guard while the comment claims the typestate guard is retained on `build()`. The stated invariant (wrong payload) is prevented either way; if same-schema requiredness is meant to extend to enqueue omission, add one line to §1's binding rules.\n- **\"proven safe\" in the `createWorkersContract` paragraph.** The constraint argument is compile-time only; wire soundness rests on the \"contract soundness tests\" named in the risk register.
